Task instruction

## EVALUATION CONTEXT

You are being evaluated on a software engineering task. Your performance will be measured by:
- Whether the existing test suite COMPILES without errors
- Whether the failing tests PASS after your implementation
- Whether you implement the EXACT interface that the tests expect

**CRITICAL: You MUST run the test suite BEFORE making any code changes.**

The test compilation errors and failures tell you exactly what interfaces, method names, and types to implement. If you implement code before running tests, you will guess wrong about method names and fail the evaluation.

**Required workflow:**
1. FIRST: Run the full test suite to see compilation errors and failures
2. THEN: Read the test files to understand exact interface requirements
3. THEN: Implement the fix matching the exact interface from tests
4. FINALLY: Run tests again to verify they pass

# big-code-flink-arch-001: Flink Checkpoint Coordination Architecture

## Task

Map the Flink checkpoint coordination architecture: how the JobManager triggers a checkpoint, propagates barriers through the task graph, and coordinates acknowledgments. Trace the complete distributed two-phase commit from CheckpointCoordinator through barrier injection, barrier alignment/processing at downstream tasks, state snapshot, acknowledgment, to CompletedCheckpoint.

## Context

- **Repository**: apache/flink (Java, ~3.5M LOC)
- **Category**: Architectural Understanding
- **Difficulty**: hard
- **Subsystem Focus**: flink-runtime/checkpoint/ and flink-streaming-java/runtime/io/checkpointing/

## Requirements

1. Identify all relevant components in the checkpoint coordination flow (JobManager-side coordinator, RPC dispatch, barrier events, barrier handlers, state snapshot, acknowledgment)
2. Trace the dependency chain from CheckpointCoordinator.triggerCheckpoint() through barrier propagation to CompletedCheckpoint
3. Document the aligned vs unaligned checkpoint barrier handling (SingleCheckpointBarrierHandler vs CheckpointBarrierTracker)
4. Explain the PendingCheckpoint lifecycle and the ack-based completion protocol

## Expected Output

Write your analysis to `/logs/agent/solution.md` with the following structure:

```
## Files Examined
- path/to/file1.ext — role in architecture
- path/to/file2.ext — role in architecture
...

## Dependency Chain
1. Entry point: path/to/entry.ext
2. Calls: path/to/next.ext (via function/method name)
3. Delegates to: path/to/impl.ext
...

## Analysis
[Detailed architectural analysis including:
- Design patterns identified
- Component responsibilities
- Data flow description
- Interface contracts between components]

## Summary
[Concise 2-3 sentence summary answering the task question]
```

## Evaluation Criteria

- File recall: Did you find the correct set of architecturally relevant files?
- Dependency accuracy: Did you trace the correct dependency/call chain?
- Architectural coherence: Did you correctly identify the design patterns and component relationships?

   836	    private CompletableFuture<Void> triggerTasks(
   837	            CheckpointTriggerRequest request, long timestamp, PendingCheckpoint checkpoint) {
   838	        // no exception, no discarding, everything is OK
   839	        final long checkpointId = checkpoint.getCheckpointID();
   840	
   841	        final SnapshotType type;
   842	        if (this.forceFullSnapshot && !request.props.isSavepoint()) {
   843	            type = FULL_CHECKPOINT;
   844	        } else {
   845	            type = request.props.getCheckpointType();
   846	        }
   847	
   848	        final CheckpointOptions checkpointOptions =
   849	                CheckpointOptions.forConfig(
   850	                        type,
   851	                        checkpoint.getCheckpointStorageLocation().getLocationReference(),
   852	                        isExactlyOnceMode,
   853	                        unalignedCheckpointsEnabled,
   854	                        alignedCheckpointTimeout);
   855	
   856	        // send messages to the tasks to trigger their checkpoints
   857	        List<CompletableFuture<Acknowledge>> acks = new ArrayList<>();
   858	        for (Execution execution : checkpoint.getCheckpointPlan().getTasksToTrigger()) {
   859	            if (request.props.isSynchronous()) {
   860	                acks.add(
   861	                        execution.triggerSynchronousSavepoint(
   862	                                checkpointId, timestamp, checkpointOptions));
   863	            } else {
   864	                acks.add(execution.triggerCheckpoint(checkpointId, timestamp, checkpointOptions));
   865	            }
   866	        }
   867	        return FutureUtils.waitForAll(acks);
   868	    }

  1100	    private boolean maybeCompleteCheckpoint(PendingCheckpoint checkpoint) {
  1101	        synchronized (lock) {
  1102	            if (checkpoint.isFullyAcknowledged()) {
  1103	                try {
  1104	                    // we need to check inside the lock for being shutdown as well,
  1105	                    // otherwise we get races and invalid error log messages.
  1106	                    if (shutdown) {
  1107	                        return false;
  1108	                    }
  1109	                    completePendingCheckpoint(checkpoint);
  1110	                } catch (CheckpointException ce) {
  1111	                    onTriggerFailure(checkpoint, ce);
  1112	                    return false;
  1113	                }
  1114	            }
  1115	        }
  1116	        return true;
  1117	    }

  1361	     *
  1362	     * @param pendingCheckpoint to complete
  1363	     * @throws CheckpointException if the completion failed
  1364	     */
  1365	    private void completePendingCheckpoint(PendingCheckpoint pendingCheckpoint)
  1366	            throws CheckpointException {
  1367	        final long checkpointId = pendingCheckpoint.getCheckpointID();
  1368	        final CompletedCheckpoint completedCheckpoint;
  1369	        final CompletedCheckpoint lastSubsumed;
  1370	        final CheckpointProperties props = pendingCheckpoint.getProps();
  1371	
  1372	        completedCheckpointStore.getSharedStateRegistry().checkpointCompleted(checkpointId);
  1373	
  1374	        try {
  1375	            completedCheckpoint = finalizeCheckpoint(pendingCheckpoint);
  1376	
  1377	            // the pending checkpoint must be discarded after the finalization
  1378	            Preconditions.checkState(pendingCheckpoint.isDisposed() && completedCheckpoint != null);
  1379	
  1380	            if (!props.isSavepoint()) {
  1381	                lastSubsumed =
  1382	                        addCompletedCheckpointToStoreAndSubsumeOldest(
  1383	                                checkpointId, completedCheckpoint, pendingCheckpoint);
  1384	            } else {
  1385	                lastSubsumed = null;
  1386	            }
  1387	
  1388	            reportCompletedCheckpoint(completedCheckpoint);
  1389	            pendingCheckpoint.getCompletionFuture().complete(completedCheckpoint);
  1390	        } catch (Exception exception) {
  1391	            // For robustness reasons, we need catch exception and try marking the checkpoint
  1392	            // completed.
  1393	            pendingCheckpoint.getCompletionFuture().completeExceptionally(exception);
  1394	            throw exception;
  1395	        } finally {
  1396	            pendingCheckpoints.remove(checkpointId);
  1397	            scheduleTriggerRequest();
  1398	        }
  1399	
  1400	        cleanupAfterCompletedCheckpoint(
  1401	                pendingCheckpoint, checkpointId, completedCheckpoint, lastSubsumed, props);
  1402	    }
  1403	
  1404	    private void reportCompletedCheckpoint(CompletedCheckpoint completedCheckpoint) {
  1405	        failureManager.handleCheckpointSuccess(completedCheckpoint.getCheckpointID());
  1406	        CompletedCheckpointStats completedCheckpointStats = completedCheckpoint.getStatistic();
  1407	        if (completedCheckpointStats != null) {
  1408	            LOG.trace(
  1409	                    "Checkpoint {} size: {}Kb, duration: {}ms",
  1410	                    completedCheckpoint.getCheckpointID(),
  1411	                    completedCheckpointStats.getStateSize() == 0
  1412	                            ? 0
  1413	                            : completedCheckpointStats.getStateSize() / 1024,
  1414	                    completedCheckpointStats.getEndToEndDuration());
  1415	            // Finalize the statsCallback and give the completed checkpoint a
  1416	            // callback for discards.
  1417	            statsTracker.reportCompletedCheckpoint(completedCheckpointStats);
  1418	        }
  1419	    }
  1420	
  1421	    private void cleanupAfterCompletedCheckpoint(
  1422	            PendingCheckpoint pendingCheckpoint,
  1423	            long checkpointId,
  1424	            CompletedCheckpoint completedCheckpoint,
  1425	            CompletedCheckpoint lastSubsumed,
  1426	            CheckpointProperties props) {
  1427	
  142...

  1088	    private CompletableFuture<Acknowledge> triggerCheckpointHelper(
  1089	            long checkpointId, long timestamp, CheckpointOptions checkpointOptions) {
  1090	
  1091	        final LogicalSlot slot = assignedResource;
  1092	
  1093	        if (slot != null) {
  1094	            final TaskManagerGateway taskManagerGateway = slot.getTaskManagerGateway();
  1095	
  1096	            return taskManagerGateway.triggerCheckpoint(
  1097	                    attemptId, getVertex().getJobId(), checkpointId, timestamp, checkpointOptions);
  1098	        }
  1099	        LOG.debug(
  1100	                "The execution has no slot assigned. This indicates that the execution is no longer running.");
  1101	        return CompletableFuture.completedFuture(Acknowledge.get());
  1102	    }

  1356	     * @param checkpointID The ID identifying the checkpoint.
  1357	     * @param checkpointTimestamp The timestamp associated with the checkpoint.
  1358	     * @param checkpointOptions Options for performing this checkpoint.
  1359	     */
  1360	    public void triggerCheckpointBarrier(
  1361	            final long checkpointID,
  1362	            final long checkpointTimestamp,
  1363	            final CheckpointOptions checkpointOptions) {
  1364	
  1365	        final TaskInvokable invokable = this.invokable;
  1366	        final CheckpointMetaData checkpointMetaData =
  1367	                new CheckpointMetaData(
  1368	                        checkpointID, checkpointTimestamp, System.currentTimeMillis());
  1369	
  1370	        if (executionState == ExecutionState.RUNNING) {
  1371	            checkState(invokable instanceof CheckpointableTask, "invokable is not checkpointable");
  1372	            try {
  1373	                ((CheckpointableTask) invokable)
  1374	                        .triggerCheckpointAsync(checkpointMetaData, checkpointOptions)
  1375	                        .handle(
  1376	                                (triggerResult, exception) -> {
  1377	                                    if (exception != null || !triggerResult) {
  1378	                                        declineCheckpoint(
  1379	                                                checkpointID,

   621	            }
   622	        }

   410	                LOG.warn(
   411	                        "{}: Received EndOfPartition(-1) before completing current checkpoint {}. Skipping current checkpoint.",
   412	                        taskName,
   413	                        currentCheckpointId);
   414	                abortInternal(currentCheckpointId, CHECKPOINT_DECLINED_INPUT_END_OF_STREAM);
   415	            }
   416	        } else {
   417	            if (!isCheckpointPending()) {
   418	                return;
   419	            }
   420	
   421	            checkState(
   422	                    pendingCheckpointBarrier != null,
   423	                    "pending checkpoint barrier should not be null when"
   424	                            + " there is pending checkpoint.");
   425	
   426	            markCheckpointAlignedAndTransformState(
   427	                    channelInfo,
   428	                    pendingCheckpointBarrier,
   429	                    state -> state.endOfPartitionReceived(context, channelInfo));
   430	        }
   431	    }
   432	
   433	    @Override
   434	    public long getLatestCheckpointId() {
   435	        return currentCheckpointId;
   436	    }
   437	
   438	    @Override
   439	    public void close() throws IOException {
   440	        resetAlignmentTimer();
   441	        allBarriersReceivedFuture.cancel(false);
   442	        super.close();
   443	    }
   444	
   445	    @Override
   446	    protected boolean isCheckpointPending() {
   447	        return currentCheckpointId != lastCancelledOrCompletedCheckpointId
   448	                && currentCheckpointId >= 0;
   449	    }
   450	
   451	    private void cancelSubsumedCheckpoint(long barrierId) throws IOException {
   452	        LOG.warn(
   453	                "{}: Received checkpoint barrier for checkpoint {} before completing current checkpoint {}. "
   454	                        + "Skipping current checkpoint.",
   455	                taskName,
   456	                barrierId,
   457	                currentCheckpointId);
   458	        abortInternal(currentCheckpointId, CHECKPOINT_DECLINED_SUBSUMED);
   459	    }
   460	
   461	    public CompletableFuture<Void> getAllBarriersReceivedFuture(long checkpointId) {
   462	        if (checkpointId < currentCheckpointId || numOpenChannels == 0) {
   463	            return FutureUtils.completedVoidFuture();
   464	        }
   465	        if (checkpointId > currentCheckpointId) {
   466	            throw new IllegalStateException(
   467	                    "Checkpoint " + checkpointId + " has not been started at all");
   468	        }
   469	        return allBarriersReceivedFuture;
   470	    }

   473	    int getNumOpenChannels() {

   475	    }

    29	/**
    30	 * Represents a state in a state machine of processing a checkpoint. There are 4 base states:
    31	 *
    32	 * <ul>
    33	 *   <li>Waiting for an aligned barrier
    34	 *   <li>Collecting aligned barriers
    35	 *   <li>Waiting for an unaligned barrier
    36	 *   <li>Collecting unaligned barriers
    37	 * </ul>
    38	 *
    39	 * <p>Additionally depending on the configuration we can switch between aligned and unaligned
    40	 * actions.
    41	 */
    42	interface BarrierHandlerState {
    43	    BarrierHandlerState alignedCheckpointTimeout(
    44	            Controller controller, CheckpointBarrier checkpointBarrier)
    45	            throws IOException, CheckpointException;
    46	
    47	    BarrierHandlerState announcementReceived(
    48	            Controller controller, InputChannelInfo channelInfo, int sequenceNumber)
    49	            throws IOException;
    50	
    51	    BarrierHandlerState barrierReceived(
    52	            Controller controller,
    53	            InputChannelInfo channelInfo,
    54	            CheckpointBarrier checkpointBarrier,
    55	            boolean markChannelBlocked)
    56	            throws IOException, CheckpointException;
    57	
    58	    BarrierHandlerState abort(long cancelledId) throws IOException;
    59	
    60	    BarrierHandlerState endOfPartitionReceived(Controller controller, InputChannelInfo channelInfo)
    61	            throws IOException, CheckpointException;
    62	
    63	    /**
    64	     * An entry point for communication between {@link BarrierHandlerState} and {@link
    65	     * SingleCheckpointBarrierHandler}.
    66	     */
    67	    interface Controller {
    68	        boolean allBarriersReceived();
    69	
    70	        @Nullable
    71	        CheckpointBarrier getPendingCheckpointBarrier();
    72	
    73	        void triggerGlobalCheckpoint(CheckpointBarrier checkpointBarrier) throws IOException;
    74	
    75	        void initInputsCheckpoint(CheckpointBarrier checkpointBarrier) throws CheckpointException;
    76	
    77	        boolean isTimedOut(CheckpointBarrier barrier);
    78	    }
    79	}

    57	/**
    58	 * A CompletedCheckpoint describes a checkpoint after all required tasks acknowledged it (with their
    59	 * state) and that is considered successful. The CompletedCheckpoint class contains all the metadata
    60	 * of the checkpoint, i.e., checkpoint ID, timestamps, and the handles to all states that are part
    61	 * of the checkpoint.
    62	 *
    63	 * <h2>Size the CompletedCheckpoint Instances</h2>
    64	 *
    65	 * <p>In most cases, the CompletedCheckpoint objects are very small, because the handles to the
    66	 * checkpoint states are only pointers (such as file paths). However, the some state backend
    67	 * implementations may choose to store some payload data directly with the metadata (for example to
    68	 * avoid many small files). If those thresholds are increased to large values, the memory
    69	 * consumption of the CompletedCheckpoint objects can be significant.
    70	 *
    71	 * <h2>Metadata Persistence</h2>
    72	 *
    73	 * <p>The metadata of the CompletedCheckpoint is also persisted in an external storage system.
    74	 * Checkpoints have an external pointer, which points to the metadata. For example when storing a
    75	 * checkpoint in a file system, that poin...

  1100	    private boolean maybeCompleteCheckpoint(PendingCheckpoint checkpoint) {
  1101	        synchronized (lock) {
  1102	            if (checkpoint.isFullyAcknowledged()) {
  1103	                try {
  1104	                    // we need to check inside the lock for being shutdown as well,
  1105	                    // otherwise we get races and invalid error log messages.
  1106	                    if (shutdown) {
  1107	                        return false;
  1108	                    }
  1109	                    completePendingCheckpoint(checkpoint);
  1110	                } catch (CheckpointException ce) {

  1112	                    return false;
  1113	                }
  1114	            }
  1115	        }

  1358	     * Try to complete the given pending checkpoint.
  1359	     *
  1360	     * <p>Important: This method should only be called in the checkpoint lock scope.
  1361	     *
  1362	     * @param pendingCheckpoint to complete
  1363	     * @throws CheckpointException if the completion failed
  1364	     */
  1365	    private void completePendingCheckpoint(PendingCheckpoint pendingCheckpoint)
  1366	            throws CheckpointException {
  1367	        final long checkpointId = pendingCheckpoint.getCheckpointID();
  1368	        final CompletedCheckpoint completedCheckpoint;
  1369	        final CompletedCheckpoint lastSubsumed;
  1370	        final CheckpointProperties props = pendingCheckpoint.getProps();
  1371	
  1372	        completedCheckpointStore.getSharedStateRegistry().checkpointCompleted(checkpointId);
  1373	
  1374	        try {
  1375	            completedCheckpoint = finalizeCheckpoint(pendingCheckpoint);
  1376	
  1377	            // the pending checkpoint must be discarded after the finalization
  1378	            Preconditions.checkState(pendingCheckpoint.isDisposed() && completedCheckpoint != null);
  1379

  1381	                lastSubsumed =
  1382	                        addCompletedCheckpointToStoreAndSubsumeOldest(
  1383	                                checkpointId, completedCheckpoint, pendingCheckpoint);
  1384	            } else {
  1385	                lastSubsumed = null;
  1386	            }
  1387	
  1388	            reportCompletedCheckpoint(completedCheckpoint);
  1389	            pendingCheckpoint.getCompletionFuture().complete(completedCheckpoint);
  1390	        } catch (Exception exception) {
  1391	            // For robustness reasons, we need catch exception and try marking the checkpoint
  1392	            // completed.
  1393	            pendingCheckpoint.getCompletionFuture().completeExceptionally(exception);
  1394	            throw exception;
  1395	        } finally {
  1396	            pendingCheckpoints.remove(checkpointId);
  1397	            scheduleTriggerRequest();
  1398	        }
  1399	
  1400	        cleanupAfterCompletedCheckpoint(
  1401	                pendingCheckpoint, checkpointId, completedCheckpoint, lastSubsumed, props);
  1402	    }

    44	import static org.apache.flink.util.Preconditions.checkNotNull;
    45	import static org.apache.flink.util.Preconditions.checkState;
    46	
    47	/**
    48	 * The {@link CheckpointBarrierTracker} keeps track of what checkpoint barriers have been received
    49	 * from which input channels. Once it has observed all checkpoint barriers for a checkpoint ID, it
    50	 * notifies its listener of a completed checkpoint.
    51	 *
    52	 * <p>Unlike the {@link SingleCheckpointBarrierHandler}, the BarrierTracker does not block the input
    53	 * channels that have sent barriers, so it cannot be used to gain "exactly-once" processing
    54	 * guarantees. It can, however, be used to gain "at least once" processing guarantees.
    55	 *
    56	 * <p>NOTE: This implementation strictly assumes that newer checkpoints have higher checkpoint IDs.
    57	 */
    58	@Internal
    59	public class CheckpointBarrierTracker extends CheckpointBarrierHandler {
    60	
    61	    private static final Logger LOG = LoggerFactory.getLogger(CheckpointBarrierTracker.class);

   100	    /**
   101	     * Eagerly pulls and processes all priority events. Must be called from task thread.
   102	     *
   103	     * <p>Basic assumption is that no priority event needs to be handled by the {@link
   104	     * StreamTaskNetworkInput}.
   105	     */
   106	    private void processPriorityEvents() throws IOException, InterruptedException {
   107	        // check if the priority event is still not processed (could have been pulled before mail
   108	        // was being executed)
   109	        boolean hasPriorityEvent = inputGate.getPriorityEventAvailableFuture().isDone();
   110	        while (hasPriorityEvent) {
   111	            // process as many priority events as possible
   112	            final Optional<BufferOrEvent> bufferOrEventOpt = pollNext();
   113	            if (!bufferOrEventOpt.isPresent()) {
   114	                break;
   115	            }
   116	            final BufferOrEvent bufferOrEvent = bufferOrEventOpt.get();
   117	            checkState(bufferOrEvent.hasPriority(), "Should only poll priority events");
   118	            hasPriorityEvent = bufferOrEvent.morePriorityEvents();
   119	        }
   120	
   121	        // re-enqueue mail to process future priority events
   122	        waitForPriorityEvents(inputGate, mailboxExecutor);
   123	    }

   284	        // records/watermarks/timers/callbacks.
   285	        // We generally try to emit the checkpoint barrier as soon as possible to not affect
   286	        // downstream

   528	    public void initInputsCheckpoint(long id, CheckpointOptions checkpointOptions)
   529	            throws CheckpointException {
   530	        if (checkpointOptions.isUnalignedCheckpoint()) {
   531	            channelStateWriter.start(id, checkpointOptions);
   532	
   533	            prepareInflightDataSnapshot(id);
   534	        } else if (checkpointOptions.isTimeoutable()) {
   535	            // The output buffer may need to be snapshotted, so start the channelStateWriter here.
   536	            channelStateWriter.start(id, checkpointOptions);
   537	            channelStateWriter.finishInput(id);
   538	        }
   539	    }
   540	
   541	    public void waitForPendingCheckpoints() throws Exception {
   542	        if (!enableCheckpointAfterTasksFinished) {
   543	            return;
   544	        }
   545	
   546	        List<AsyncCheckpointRunnable> asyncCheckpointRunnables;
   547	        synchronized (lock) {
   548	            asyncCheckpointRunnables = new ArrayList<>(checkpoints.values());
   549	        }
   550	
   551	        // Waits for each checkpoint independently.
   552	        asyncCheckpointRunnables.forEach(
   553	                ar -> {
   554	                    try {
   555	                        ar.getFinishedFuture().get();
   556	                    } catch (Exception e) {
   557	                        LOG.debug(
   558	                                "Async runnable for checkpoint "
   559	                                        + ar.getCheckpointId()
   560	                                        + " throws exception and exit",
   561	                                e);
   562	                    }
   563	                });
   564	    }
   565	
   566	    @Override
   567	    public void close() throws IOException {
   568	        cancelAlignmentTimer();
   569	        cancel();

# Apache Flink Checkpoint Coordination Architecture

## Overview

Flink's checkpoint system implements Chandy-Lamport distributed snapshots adapted for streaming
dataflows. The protocol is driven by a single `CheckpointCoordinator` on the JobManager and
propagated through the operator graph via in-band `CheckpointBarrier` events. This document traces
the complete flow from coordinator trigger through barrier propagation to `CompletedCheckpoint`,
covering both aligned and unaligned barrier handling and the full `PendingCheckpoint` lifecycle.

---

## 1. Coordinator Trigger — JobManager Side

### 1.1 Entry Points

```
CheckpointCoordinator.triggerCheckpoint(isPeriodic)
  └─ triggerCheckpointFromCheckpointThread(checkpointProperties, null, isPeriodic)
       └─ timer.execute(() -> triggerCheckpoint(props, location, isPeriodic))
```

All checkpoint initiation — periodic, manual, and savepoint — funnels through
`triggerCheckpointFromCheckpointThread`, which posts the actual trigger onto the coordinator's
dedicated timer thread. This serializes all checkpoint decisions onto a single thread, avoiding
lock contention with acknowledgment processing.

### 1.2 Request Admission — CheckpointRequestDecider

Before any work begins, `chooseRequestToExecute(request)` consults `CheckpointRequestDecider`,
which enforces:

- **`maxConcurrentCheckpoints`** — rejects new periodic requests if `isTriggering` is true.
- **`minPauseBetweenCheckpoints`** — compares `clock.relativeTimeMillis()` against
  `lastCheckpointCompletionRelativeTime`; queues or drops the request if the pause has not elapsed.
- **Savepoint priority** — savepoints bypass the concurrent-checkpoint limit and are never dropped.

Only if the decider returns a request does `startTriggeringCheckpoint` proceed.

### 1.3 startTriggeringCheckpoint — Async Pipeline

`startTriggeringCheckpoint` constructs a `CompletableFuture` chain that executes across two thread
pools: the coordinator's `executor` (I/O-capable) and the `timer` ...

  1100	    private boolean maybeCompleteCheckpoint(PendingCheckpoint checkpoint) {
  1101	        synchronized (lock) {
  1102	            if (checkpoint.isFullyAcknowledged()) {
  1103	                try {
  1104	                    // we need to check inside the lock for being shutdown as well,
  1105	                    // otherwise we get races and invalid error log messages.
  1106	                    if (shutdown) {
  1107	                        return false;
  1108	                    }
  1109	                    completePendingCheckpoint(checkpoint);
  1110	                } catch (CheckpointException ce) {
  1111	                    onTriggerFailure(checkpoint, ce);
  1112	                    return false;
  1113	                }
  1114	            }
  1115	        }
  1116	        return true;
  1117	    }

  1100	    private boolean maybeCompleteCheckpoint(PendingCheckpoint checkpoint) {
  1101	        synchronized (lock) {
  1102	            if (checkpoint.isFullyAcknowledged()) {
  1103	                try {
  1104	                    // we need to check inside the lock for being shutdown as well,
  1105	                    // otherwise we get races and invalid error log messages.
  1106	                    if (shutdown) {
  1107	                        return false;
  1108	                    }
  1109	                    completePendingCheckpoint(checkpoint);
  1110	                } catch (CheckpointException ce) {
  1111	                    onTriggerFailure(checkpoint, ce);
  1112	                    return false;
  1113	                }
  1114	            }
  1115	        }
  1116	        return true;
  1117	    }

#286 assistant/text 
I am thinking...: Now I have all the information I need to write a comprehensive analysis. Let me organize the key findings:

1. **CheckpointCoordinator.triggerCheckpoint()** flow:
   - `triggerCheckpoint(isPeriodic)` → `triggerCheckpointFromCheckpointThread()` → timer.execute
   - `triggerCheckpoint(props, location, isPeriodic)` → `chooseRequestToExecute(request)` via `CheckpointRequestDecider` (handles maxConcurrentCheckpoints, minPauseBetweenCheckpoints)
   - `startTriggeringCheckpoint()`:
     - `checkpointPlanCalculator.calculateCheckpointPlan()` (async)
     - `checkpointIdCounter.getAndIncrement()` (async, in executor)
     - `createPendingCheckpoint()` (in timer thread):
       - Creates `PendingCheckpoint` with `notYetAcknowledgedTasks` map
       - Registers `CheckpointCanceller` via `timer.schedule(checkpointTimeout)`
       - Adds to `pendingCheckpoints` map
     - `initializeCheckpointLocation()` (async, in executor)
     - `OperatorCoordinatorCheckpoints.triggerAndAcknowledgeAllCoordinatorCheckpointsWithCompletion()` (coordinator state)

     1	# Apache Flink Checkpoint Coordination Architecture
     2	
     3	## Overview
     4	
     5	Flink's checkpoint system implements Chandy-Lamport distributed snapshots adapted for streaming
     6	dataflows. The protocol is driven by a single `CheckpointCoordinator` on the JobManager and
     7	propagated through the operator graph via in-band `CheckpointBarrier` events. This document traces
     8	the complete flow from coordinator trigger through barrier propagation to `CompletedCheckpoint`,
     9	covering both aligned and unaligned barrier handling and the full `PendingCheckpoint` lifecycle.
    10	
    11	---
    12	
    13	## 1. Coordinator Trigger — JobManager Side
    14	
    15	### 1.1 Entry Points
    16	
    17	```
    18	CheckpointCoordinator.triggerCheckpoint(isPeriodic)
    19	  └─ triggerCheckpointFromCheckpointThread(checkpointProperties, null, isPeriodic)
    20	       └─ timer.execute(() -> triggerCheckpoint(props, location, isPeriodic))
    21	```
    22	
    23	All checkpoint initiation — periodic, manual, and savepoint — funnels through
    24	`triggerCheckpointFromCheckpointThread`, which...

  "thought": "Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the Flink checkpoint coordination architecture. Let me synthesize all the information I've gathered:\n\n## Complete Architecture Flow\n\n### 1. JobManager Side - Trigger Phase\n\n**CheckpointCoordinator** (flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointCoordinator.java)\n- Entry point: `triggerCheckpoint(boolean isPeriodic)` → `triggerCheckpointFromCheckpointThread()` → `triggerCheckpoint(props, location, isPeriodic)`\n- Creates `CheckpointTriggerRequest`, passes to `chooseRequestToExecute()` → `startTriggeringCheckpoint()`\n- `startTriggeringCheckpoint()`:\n  1. Calls `checkpointPlanCalculator.calculateCheckpointPlan()` to determine which tasks to trigger/wait for\n  2. Gets checkpoint ID from `checkpointIdCounter.getAndIncrement()`\n  3. Creates `PendingCheckpoint` via `createPendingCheckpoint()` - registers in `pendingCheckpoints` map, schedules timeout via `CheckpointCanceller`\n  4. Initializes checkpoint storage location via `initializeCheckpointLocation()`\n  5. Triggers OperatorCoordinator checkpoints via `OperatorCoordinatorCheckpoints.triggerAndAcknowledgeAllCoordinatorCheckpointsWithCompletion()`\n  6. Snapshots master hook states via `snapshotMasterState()`\n  7. After all coordinator/master states complete, calls `triggerCheckpointRequest()` → `triggerTasks()`\n- `triggerTasks()`: For each source task in `checkpoint.getCheckpointPlan().getTasksToTrigger()`, calls `execution.triggerCheckpoint(checkpointId, timestamp, checkpointOptions)`\n\n### 2. RPC Dispatch - Trigger to TaskManager\n\n**Execution** (flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/executiongraph/Execution.java)\n- `triggerCheckpoint()` → gets `TaskManagerGateway` from slot → calls `taskManagerGateway.triggerCheckpoint(attemptId, jobId, checkpointId, timestamp, checkpointOptions)`\n\n**TaskManagerGateway** (interface) → **RpcTaskManagerGateway** (implementation)\n- `RpcTaskManagerGateway.triggerCheckpoint()` → delegates to `TaskExecutorGateway.triggerCheckpoint()` (RPC call to TaskExecutor)\n\n**TaskExecutorGateway** (interface) → **TaskExecutor** (implementation)\n- Receives RPC, finds the Task, calls `task.triggerCheckpointBarrier(checkpointId, timestamp, checkpointOptions)`\n\n### 3. Task Side - Barrier Injection\n\n**StreamTask** (flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/streaming/runtime/tasks/StreamTask.java)\n- `triggerCheckpointAsync()` → `performCheckpoint()` → `subtaskCheckpointCoordinator.checkpointState()`\n\n**SubtaskCheckpointCoordinatorImpl** (flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/streaming/runtime/tasks/SubtaskCheckpointCoordinatorImpl.java)\n- `checkpointState()` steps:\n  1. `operatorChain.prepareSnapshotPreBarrier()` - pre-barrier work\n  2. Creates `CheckpointBarrier` and calls `operatorChain.broadcastEvent(checkpointBarrier)` - **injects barrier into output channels**\n  3. Registers alignment timer (for alternating checkpoints)\n  4. For unaligned: `channelStateWriter.finishOutput()` - captures in-flight output buffers\n  5. `takeSnapshotSync()` - synchronous snapshot of operator state via `operatorChain.snapshotState()`\n  6. `finishAndReportAsync()` - submits `AsyncCheckpointRunnable` to async thread pool\n\n### 4. Downstream Task - Barrier Processing\n\n**CheckpointedInputGate** wraps the input gate and routes barrier events to the `CheckpointBarrierHandler`.\n\n**CheckpointBarrierHandler** (abstract base class)\n- `notifyCheckpoint()` → calls `toNotifyOnCheckpoint.triggerCheckpointOnBarrier()` (which is the StreamTask)\n\nTwo implementations:\n\n**CheckpointBarrierTracker** (AT_LEAST_ONCE mode):\n- Does NOT block input channels\n- Tracks barriers per checkpoint ID using `CheckpointBarrierCount` queue\n- When all barriers received for a checkpoint ID → `triggerCheckpointOnAligned()` → `notifyCheckpoint()`\n- Can track up to 50 concurrent checkpoints\n\n**SingleCheckpointBarrierHandler** (EXACTLY_ONCE mode):\n- Blocks input

# Flink Checkpoint Coordination Architecture

## Files Examined

- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointCoordinator.java` — Central JobManager-side coordinator: triggers checkpoints, tracks pending checkpoints, receives acknowledgments, drives completion
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/PendingCheckpoint.java` — Mutable in-flight checkpoint state: tracks which tasks/coordinators/master-hooks have not yet acknowledged; finalizes into CompletedCheckpoint
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CompletedCheckpoint.java` — Immutable record of a successfully completed checkpoint with all operator state handles and external metadata pointer
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointCoordinatorGateway.java` — RPC interface through which TaskManagers send `acknowledgeCheckpoint` and `declineCheckpoint` messages back to the JobMaster
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointPlan.java` — Describes which tasks to trigger (sources), which to wait for (all), and which to commit to after completion
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/DefaultCheckpointPlanCalculator.java` — Computes the CheckpointPlan from the ExecutionGraph topology
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/OperatorCoordinatorCheckpoints.java` — Triggers and acknowledges OperatorCoordinator-side checkpoints before task barriers are sent
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointRequestDecider.java` — Decides whether a new checkpoint trigger request should execute immediately or be queued, enforcing max-concurrent and min-pause constraints
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointFailureManager.java` — Tracks checkpoint failures and decides when to fail the job
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/executiongraph/Execution.java` — Represents a running task attempt; dispatches `triggerCheckpoint` RPC to the TaskManager via `TaskManagerGateway`
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/jobmaster/RpcTaskManagerGateway.java` — Implements `TaskManagerGateway` by delegating to `TaskExecutorGateway` (Akka/Pekko RPC)
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/jobmanager/slots/TaskManagerGateway.java` — Interface for JobManager→TaskManager RPC calls including `triggerCheckpoint`
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/taskexecutor/TaskExecutorGateway.java` — Interface for the TaskExecutor RPC endpoint; receives `triggerCheckpoint` from the JobMaster
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/streaming/runtime/tasks/StreamTask.java` — Base class for all streaming tasks; implements `CheckpointableTask`; routes `triggerCheckpointOnBarrier` into `SubtaskCheckpointCoordinatorImpl.checkpointState()`
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/streaming/runtime/tasks/SubtaskCheckpointCoordinatorImpl.java` — Task-side checkpoint orchestrator: broadcasts the barrier downstream, takes synchronous state snapshots, submits async finalization
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/streaming/runtime/tasks/AsyncCheckpointRunnable.java` — Async runnable that finalizes operator snapshot futures and calls `reportCompletedSnapshotStates` to send the ACK

`SubtaskCheckpointCoordinatorImpl`, `AsyncCheckpointRunnable`, `TaskStateManagerImpl`, `CheckpointBarrierHandler`, `SingleCheckpointBarrierHandler`, `CheckpointBarrierTracker`, `CheckpointedInputGate`, `InputProcessorUtil`, and all `BarrierHandlerState` implementations.\n\n**Dependency Chain (17 steps):** Traced from `CheckpointCoordinator.triggerCheckpoint()` → plan calculation → `PendingCheckpoint` creation → OperatorCoordinator/master-hook snapshots → `Execution.triggerCheckpoint()` RPC → `SubtaskCheckpointCoordinatorImpl.checkpointState()` → barrier broadcast → downstream `CheckpointedInputGate` → `CheckpointBarrierHandler.processBarrier()` → `triggerCheckpointOnBarrier()` → `AsyncCheckpointRunnable` → `TaskStateManagerImpl.acknowledgeCheckpoint()` RPC → `CheckpointCoordinator.receiveAcknowledgeMessage()` → `PendingCheckpoint.acknowledgeTask()` → `completePendingCheckpoint()` → `CompletedCheckpoint` creation and persistence → `sendAcknowledgeMessages()`.\n\n**Aligned vs. Unaligned:** `SingleCheckpointBarrierHandler` uses a `BarrierHandlerState` state machine with three modes: aligned (blocks channels, waits for all barriers), unaligned (triggers immediately on first announcement, captures in-flight buffers), and alternating (starts aligned, times out to unaligned). `CheckpointBarrierTracker` provides AT_LEAST_ONCE semantics by counting barriers without blocking.\n\n**PendingCheckpoint Lifecycle:** Created with timeout → accumulates ACKs via `acknowledgeTask()` → `isFullyAcknowledged()` triggers `finalizeCheckpoint()` → metadata written → `CompletedCheckpoint` stored in HA store → `onCompletionPromise` fulfilled → `notifyCheckpointComplete` broadcast to all tasks.\n\n**Design Patterns:** Distributed 2PC, State Machine (BarrierHandlerState), Observer/Listener (CheckpointableTask), Gateway/RPC abstraction (three gateway layers), Future-based async pipeline, Template Method."
}

# Flink Checkpoint Coordination Architecture

## Files Examined

- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointCoordinator.java` — Central JobManager-side coordinator: triggers checkpoints, tracks pending checkpoints, receives acknowledgments, drives completion
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/PendingCheckpoint.java` — Mutable in-flight checkpoint state: tracks which tasks/coordinators/master-hooks have not yet acknowledged; finalizes into CompletedCheckpoint
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CompletedCheckpoint.java` — Immutable record of a successfully completed checkpoint with all operator state handles and external metadata pointer
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointCoordinatorGateway.java` — RPC interface through which TaskManagers send `acknowledgeCheckpoint` and `declineCheckpoint` messages back to the JobMaster
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointPlan.java` — Describes which tasks to trigger (sources), which to wait for (all), and which to commit to after completion
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/DefaultCheckpointPlanCalculator.java` — Computes the CheckpointPlan from the ExecutionGraph topology
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/OperatorCoordinatorCheckpoints.java` — Triggers and acknowledges OperatorCoordinator-side checkpoints before task barriers are sent
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointRequestDecider.java` — Decides whether a new checkpoint trigger request should execute immediately or be queued, enforcing max-concurrent and min-pause constraints
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointFailureManager.java` — Tracks checkpoint failures and decides when to fail the job
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/execut...

#1 system/init 
You are OpenHands agent, a helpful AI assistant that can interact with a computer to solve tasks.

<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.

## Complete Architecture Flow

### 1. JobManager Side - Trigger Phase

**CheckpointCoordinator** (flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointCoordinator.java)
- Entry point: `triggerCheckpoint(boolean isPeriodic)` → `triggerCheckpointFromCheckpointThread()` → `triggerCheckpoint(props, location, isPeriodic)`
- Creates `CheckpointTriggerRequest`, passes to `chooseRequestToExecute()` → `startTriggeringCheckpoint()`
- `startTriggeringCheckpoint()`:
  1. Calls `checkpointPlanCalculator.calculateCheckpointPlan()` to determine which tasks to trigger/wait for
  2. Gets checkpoint ID from `checkpointIdCounter.getAndIncrement()`
  3. Creates `PendingCheckpoint` via `createPendingCheckpoint()` - registers in `pendingCheckpoints` map, schedules timeout via `CheckpointCanceller`
  4. Initializes checkpoint storage location via `initializeCheckpointLocation()`
  5. Triggers OperatorCoordinator checkpoints via `OperatorCoordinatorCheckpoints.triggerAndAcknowledgeAllCoordinatorCheckpointsWithCompletion()`

## Summary of Findings

### Complete Checkpoint Coordination Flow

**Phase 1 — Trigger & Barrier Propagation:**
1. `CheckpointCoordinator.triggerCheckpoint()` creates a `PendingCheckpoint`, triggers OperatorCoordinator/master-hook snapshots, then sends `triggerCheckpoint` RPCs to source tasks via `Execution` → `TaskManagerGateway` → `TaskExecutorGateway`
2. Source tasks receive the trigger in `StreamTask`, which calls `SubtaskCheckpointCoordinatorImpl.checkpointState()` — this broadcasts a `CheckpointBarrier` downstream, takes a synchronous state snapshot, and submits an `AsyncCheckpointRunnable`
3. Downstream tasks receive barriers through `CheckpointedInputGate` → `CheckpointBarrierHandler`, which either aligns (blocks channels) or tracks (counts) barriers before triggering their own snapshots

**Phase 2 — Acknowledgment & Completion:**
4. `AsyncCheckpointRunnable` finalizes snapshot futures and calls `TaskStateManagerImpl.reportTaskStateSnapshots()` → `checkpointResponder.acknowledgeCheckpoint()` RPC back to the JobMaster
5. `CheckpointCoordinator.receiveAcknowledgeMessage()` calls `PendingCheckpoint.ack...

# Flink Checkpoint Coordination Architecture

## Files Examined

- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointCoordinator.java` — Central JobManager-side coordinator: triggers checkpoints, tracks pending checkpoints, receives acknowledgments, drives completion
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/PendingCheckpoint.java` — Mutable in-flight checkpoint state: tracks which tasks/coordinators/master-hooks have not yet acknowledged; finalizes into CompletedCheckpoint
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CompletedCheckpoint.java` — Immutable record of a successfully completed checkpoint with all operator state handles and external metadata pointer
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointCoordinatorGateway.java` — RPC interface through which TaskManagers send `acknowledgeCheckpoint` and `declineCheckpoint` messages back to the JobMaster
- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/runtime/checkpoint/CheckpointPlan.java` — Describes which tasks to trigger (sources), which to wait for (all), and which to commit to after completion
